## Correction — EasyEDA SPICE pin field is not the physical pin number

Official EasyEDA Pin Attributes distinguishes Number (physical footprint pin number) from Spice Number (simulation-model pin mapping): https://docs.easyeda.com/en/SchematicLib/SchLib-Attributes/ . Earlier reviews treated c_spicepin differences as proof of duplicate/wrong physical pins. That conclusion was overstated and is withdrawn. Do not ask the user to renumber J_TEST5/GPS/ACS/LID/ALARM physicalpins just to match c_spicepin. Retain printed physical pin order and actual header/module fit; nativephysicalpadmappingisnotprovenorrejectedbysimulationindexdifferencesalone. Organized review.json now records this correction without deleting original observations. This doesnotwaive true power-net/measurement-feed issues or establishphysicalPASS.

Exact organizedSVGlabelcorrections: HCWOUT+ +5V_TO_ESP32→+5V_HCW; HCWOUT−GND_TO_ESP32→GND; PZEM+VB LARGE_SCREW_+LOAD→PZEM_FUSED_POSITIVE (actualsourceafterappropriatefuse); PZEMDC− LARGE_SCREW_-LOAD→SHUNT_SOURCE_NEGATIVE (actualSOURCEside). Keep selectedboard+5Vseparatefromraw+5V_HCW and explicitlyshowretainedreviewedremovablefeedbetweenthem, not a permanent USB/external5Vjoin. Renaming alone doesnotshowthatselection/feed.

Keep organizedalarmprinted1GPIO27/2GND and matchingoffboardcallouts; do not rewire to oldguide1GND/2signalwithoutchangingbothends. CurrentPZEMcommunication+5V/GNDnamedconnectionsarecorrect; currentSVG1+5V/4GND numberingdiffersfromoldsymbol,physicalterminalnamesgovern. No GPIOchange or blanketpinrework needed. No website/firmware/hardwarechangeinthisclarification.

## 25 October — simplified DP1 daily energy tables (local revision, publication unavailable)

User rejects the earlier ideal-nameplate-hour comparison and asks for slide-ready daily generation, consumption, charging contribution, range and riding-time computations for both prototype and BEMAC. The first Calculations card now contains concise solar-only and future stationary-hybrid tables, with simple substitutions and a primary-source basis below closed disclosures. The detailed measurement derivation and 36-input historical advanced audit remain in a separate appendix; component/protection justification remains its own card. Existing 39 numbered cards, original artwork, schematics and app/firmware are preserved.

Chosen theoretical case: actual reported 400 W array, 4 PSH, 70% array-to-battery-bus yield, 10 W total average battery-side electronics budget for 24h. 70% is an engineering allowance illustrated by NREL V5 14% non-thermal loss, standard-module −0.47%/°C, an ASSUMED 45°C cell temperature and ASSUMED 90% controller efficiency: 0.86×0.906×0.90=0.701244. The NREL method informs the scenario; it does not certify actual MPT efficiency. ## 23 October — available capacitors clarified; proposed filter not installable as written

User reports a 104 ceramic, 470 µF and 1000 µF electrolytics rated 25 V, and a 100 µF / 25 V electrolytic. Quantity of 104 ceramics is not established. Standard 104 code means 100,000 pF = 100 nF = 0.1 µF; it establishes capacitance, not voltage rating. Thus the specified bypass capacitance is present, while the proposed 10 µF ceramic is not. Do not silently substitute the large electrolytics at A1: uncharacterized leakage through the existing approximately 5 kohm source resistance could create a significant offset, and time constant changes. The proposed 3.2 Hz filter remains unbuilt/unverified; no repeat capture or calibration enable is requested.

Clarify switch language: 24 V DC is the minimum proposed switch voltage rating, not a request to power the circuit at 24 V. The switch is only intended to interrupt bank positive while ACS stays powered. Existing records identify the user's CNLONQ/LONQ 4B rotary DC isolator as a possible existing switch; last guidance includes model-specific bridges and a cold terminal-path check, not a completed physical verification. Do not assume its presence means correctly wired/available or improvise terminal pairs from a generic toggle. No new purchase or LONQ rewiring is requested in this clarification. Inventory access used existing M6_ISOLATOR_CLARIFICATION_2026-10-21.md and SHARED_BATTERY_CARD_2026-10-22.md. No physical change/result/PASS.

Primary capacitance-code example: https://datasheets.kyocera-avx.com/AutoCapArray.pdf . Electrolytic leakage behavior: https://www.chemi-con.co.jp/en/faq/detail.php?id=alElectricalCh . These explain the component distinction; they do not identify the user's exact capacitor voltage rating, series or leakage.

User again receives60,000USBbytes with39,684non-ASCII/control bytes; refreshing restores valid readings. Prior reader-exception recovery missed **sustained binary input without a read exception**. Implemented bounded same-port reopen: after >=4096bytes and >=25% non-text bytes with no valid startup report for2s (or10s after a prior valid report), reopen the same selected port/settings **once per connection**, release DTR/RTS false, reset decoder/partial-line state and await a complete strict frame. Do not change baud/device, deliberately assert reset, accept corrupted bytes, close the app session or loop reopens. Commands are blocked during the internal reopen. Adapter-induced reset behavior and the physical cause of the byte corruption remain unproved. Browser/node fixtures are not physical adapter acceptance.

Confirmed code defect: `lteClockEpoch` mapped unsetmodem70/01/01 to2070. Prepared2.0.15 rejects this default and supports2024–2069; implausible UTC is emitted as null. PPP may dial with an unset modem RTC, then must obtain usable ESP32 UTC before TLS. Legacy modemHTTPS still requires its clock. HTTPS Date is strictly parsed only after the certificate-verified response and corrects >2s skew; malformed dates are ignored. TLS certificate/time checks stay enabled. LTE Start/Once now fetch backend `/api/health.server_time` and send that UTC, not browser wall time; missing server time sends no command. Current user report alone does not prove exactly which clock supplied every rejected timestamp.

409 was previously always labelled payload_identity_conflict. Server now keeps the original strict409 behavior and emits/audits distinct `stale_sequence` vs `payload_identity_conflict`;400future rejections include `future_timestamp`. Firmware decodes only bounded, whitelisted rejection codes, including chunked bodies, without logging raw replies. It does not acknowledge rejected frames or weaken replay checks. Older LTE frames arriving after a newer USB record can be rejected as stale; the user's particular409 was not previously audited, so do not claim its exact cause established. Checked47stored boot payloads with installedArduinoJson host round-trip: no numeric changes. No serialization-tolerance workaround added.

TCP errno119 in this ESP32 toolchain is EINPROGRESS; the log establishes a separate socket-connect failure, not an application400/409 or a uniquely proved supply/SIM cause. Existing bounded retries/session recovery remain. Need fresh current USB-forwarded capture to see whether it persists. No blind power/CA/APN change.

**Prepared change, not physical PASS:**2.0.8 adds opt-in`LTE_PPP_ENABLED` for installed Arduino3.3.12 only, using Arduino/esp_modem PPP SIM7600-compatible profile over the existing UART2 GPIO13/14. UART ownership transfers from HardwareSerial2 to esp_modem; raw periodic AT polling is suppressed while PPP owns it. Stop/one-shot completion releases PPP and restores prior AT UART. Existing pending periodic AT replies are drained before explicit commands, preventing an oldOK from acknowledging a new command. Tooling corrections during work: sandbox npm cache-only access required a permitted network install; Node tests required subprocess access. Windows toolchain path length prevented compiler launch until a short project-local junction was used. The compiler then exposed a C++11 constexpr limitation and `alarm` POSIX-name collision, both corrected. An additive Supabase table-permission migration corrected an ordering failure. Browser testing exposed a repeated-mode selection edge case; the app now ignores selecting the already-active mode. Browser-origin USB ingestion is used so production Secure session-cookie behavior is preserved. These initial failures are not counted as passes.

## Current physical and transport limits

- Default unknown module interfaces and powered output remain disabled. Team-reported earlier siren/MPU/switch/power success is distinct from new combined-firmware observation. No full/partial bench-ready physical verdict is earned by these tests.
- UART allocation is real source, compiled with EspSoftwareSerial; GPS stability/checksums/overflow during LTE/Wi-Fi/Modbus activity still requires the actual soak. Compilation does not prove concurrency timing.
- Wi-Fi supports rootCA-verified HTTPS and a deliberate isolated privateHTTP bench opt-in. A76XX privateHTTP fallback code is implemented but uncommissioned; public cellular cannot normally route to a private laptop IP. Carrier-specific productionHTTPS CA setup remains a precise gate; no public insecure fallback/TLS bypass is supplied.
- HW-548 active-HIGH is now supported by team-reported GPIO27 measurements; reset-safe interface remains pending. Modem connector/supply/levels, ACS conditioning/calibration and exact PZEM revision/shunt/isolation must be documented. A GPIO command is not physical siren feedback. Float/disconnect detection of analog wiring is hardware-dependent.
- Bounded important-event NVS storage is six separate event strings plus one latest volatile snapshot; trigger queue16. Capacity/write failure/real power interruption can lose data. Only real tests establish reset recovery; this work does not claim loss-free delivery.
- CPE CSV cap5,000 rows; UI history uses recent100 records, displays20 v2 snapshots. Existing paginated API/full payload JSON remain available. MeterWh/load mode are retained without inventing PV-input/battery charging/SOC.
- Wind controller, traction/BMS charging approval, GPS calibration and physical output timings remain external gates. Existing SOS/messages/roles/PWA remain in the same app; cloud deployment/provider and phone-to-PC acceptance were not performed.

## Evidence

Software fixture artifacts and final compile logs are under `docs/cpe-evidence/`. No provisioned secrets or real measurements belong in those files. Physical group results should be stored separately with tester/date/wiring/instrument/config and explicit PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED in the [consolidated acceptance guide](COMPLETE_CPE_BENCH_AND_APP_ACCEPTANCE.md).

### Subsequent compatible-driver implementation — 12 October

### Test01 new laptop bench report — identity read returns zero bytes

User supplied repeated `MPU detected=0`, all-zero invalid vectors, and `MPU_PROBE address=0x68 register_status=0 bytes=0 WHO_AM_I=unavailable profile=unknown compat_enabled=1 initialized=0 read_valid=0`. Current Test01 packaged diagnostic/modules/profile/pin headers match canonical source. This is failed acquisition, not stationary zero motion or a physical PASS. The compatible profile is already allowed in Test01; it cannot initialize without a successful identity read.

Important interpretation: the installed ESP32 Wire implementation defers a non-stop pointer write until `requestFrom`; `endTransmission(false)` can return0 without a completed physical transaction. Therefore `register_status=0` alone is **not proof of address ACK or register-write success**. Check `SCAN` (stop-terminated probes), isolate ESP+IMU, inspect soldered contacts/jumpers and verify module-pin supply/reference before attributing the failure to a particular component. Current Arduino core was found changed to3.3.12; restoration to the project-tested2.0.17 was started as a software baseline correction, not a diagnosed hardware cure. No physical upload or measurement was performed here.

### Test 06 ACS758 (HW-525) electrical characterization — 15 October

Physical inspection and multimeter testing confirmed:
- Sensor IC: genuine `ACS758LCB-050B` (Bidirectional ±50 A) on `HW-525` carrier.
- Supply requirement: At 3.3 V supply, output collapses to 0.33–0.35 V (under-voltage lockout of internal chopper amplifier). At 5.0 V supply (ESP32 5V/VIN), zero-current quiescent output `OU1` measures exactly **2.50 V** ($V_{CC}/2$).
- Electrical gate verification: At 5.0 V supply, output swings up to 4.5 V at +50 A. Connecting raw `OU1` directly to ESP32 GPIO34 is unsafe. Voltage conditioning (resistor divider) is required before connecting to GPIO34. Raw `OU1` kept disconnected from GPIO34.
Verdict: **Electrical characteristics verified; raw GPIO34 connection prohibited without divider**.

### Test 08b A7670E Cat-1 LTE Modem RF Link & Packet Tunnel PASS — 18 October

Physical testing of the SIMCom A7670E Cat-1 LTE cellular modem connected to ESP32 UART2 (GPIO13 RX, GPIO14 TX at 115200 baud) confirmed:
- Power stabilization: Transmit burst brownout during network registration resolved by adding a 470 µF 25V low-ESR electrolytic capacitor directly across the ESP32 5V and GND rail.
- AT communication & SIM status: Clean bidirectional UART exchange confirmed (`AT=1`, `+CPIN: READY`, `SMS DONE`, `PB DONE`).
- RF Network Registration: After antenna orientation, modem locked to LTE network with `CSQ 18` (approx. -77 dBm, strong signal).
- Packet Data Tunnel: Carrier network confirmed active EPS and ME packet data network contexts (`+CGEV: EPS PDN ACT 1` and `+CGEV: ME PDN ACT 8,1`), with `attached=1`.
Verdict: **Test 08b A7670E Cat-1 LTE Modem physical RF attachment and network packet tunnel PASS**.

### Test 09b PZEM-017 DC Energy Meter & TTL-to-RS485 v2.0 PASS — 18 October

Physical testing of the PZEM-017 DC Energy Meter via the TTL-to-RS485 v2.0 automatic direction flow transceiver on ESP32 UART1 (GPIO16 RX, GPIO17 TX at 9600 baud 8N2) confirmed:
- Transceiver Wiring: Silkscreen directional indicators verified (`TXD -> RXD` connects module TXD to ESP32 RX GPIO16; `RXD <- TXD` connects module RXD from ESP32 TX GPIO17).
- Optocoupled vs Measurement Power Domains: Green 4-pin plug Pin 1 (5V) and Pin 4 (GND) provide isolated power to the optical interface. Measurement terminals 4 (+) and 3 (-) monitor DC traction/bench supply (12V adapter), while Micro-USB auxiliary port ensures internal MCU boot.
- Auto-Configuration of 50A Shunt: Meter factory default was holding register 0x0003 = `0x0000` (100A). Firmware dynamically issued Function 06 write (`01 06 00 03 00 01 B8 0A`), meter acknowledged and burned `0x0001` (50A) to non-volatile EEPROM; subsequent readback confirmed `01 03 02 00 01 79 84` -> **`shunt50_verified=1`**.
- Live Telemetry Ingestion: Meter streamed full 21-byte Function 04 frames (`01 04 10 04 CD 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00 00 00 00 4A 7A`) with valid CRC (`4A 7A`) -> **`valid=1`**.
  - Register 0x0000 (Voltage): `0x04CD = 1229` -> **`12.29 V`** (exact match to the 12V bench adapter).
  - Register 0x0004 (Energy): `1 Wh`.
  - Display formatting: Replaced variadic `Serial.printf` with type-safe `Serial.print` to prevent Xtensa LX6 float/double stack alignment shifting.
Verdict: **Test 09b PZEM-017 Modbus RTU communication, 50A shunt EEPROM configuration, and live voltage measurement PASS**.

### Test 06 ACS758 Voltage Divider Conditioning & GPIO34 Verification PASS — 18 October

Physical installation and multimeter validation of the 2:1 resistive divider (2× 10 kΩ) for the ACS758LCB-050B on ESP32 GPIO34 confirmed:
- Sensor supply & quiescent voltage: With ESP32 USB power, `ACS VCC` measures **4.70 V** and raw sensor output `OU1` measures **2.30 V** (exact ratiometric match: $4.70\text{ V} / 2 = 2.35\text{ V}$).
- Conditioned midpoint: Multimeter at ESP32 GPIO34 measures **1.17 V** (exact 2:1 division of 2.34 V).
- Electrical gate verification: GPIO34 voltage (1.17 V at zero current, ~2.2 V at +50 A max load) is safely below ESP32 3.3 V absolute maximum.
- Firmware ingestion: ESP32 reads GPIO34 within `safe_range=1` with multi-sample smoothing. Zero-load baseline set to `ACS_ZERO_V 2.34f`, with `ACS_CALIBRATED false` (honestly reporting `uncalibrated` / `null` current in telemetry pending calibrated bench load testing).
Verdict: **Test 06 ACS758 2:1 voltage divider conditioning and GPIO34 electrical safety PASS**.

### Test 11 All-Module Concurrent Integration & Bench Soak — 18 October

Physical execution of `11_combined_all_sensors_soak.ino` running all CPE subsystems concurrently:
- **MPU6500-Compatible IMU**: Streams live 3-axis acceleration (`ax ~ -1.54 m/s²`, `ay ~ 0.12 m/s²`, `az ~ 9.44 m/s²`) and 3-axis gyro (`gx ~ -3.4°/s`, `gy ~ 8.8°/s`, `gz ~ -1.3°/s`). Periodic check stabilized to single `matches(0x75, 0x70)` WHO_AM_I identity check, eliminating I2C register contention with ADS1115.
- **Tamper Microswitches**: Debounced polling on GPIO25 (Lid) and GPIO26 (Mount) with `INPUT_PULLUP`. Secure when grounded (`LOW`), tampered when open (`HIGH`).
- **ADS1115 16-Bit ADC**: I2C `0x48` active, reading `A0 = 0.5826 V (4661)` from low-voltage divider reference; A1, A2, A3 grounded (`-0.0005 V`).
- **ACS758 Current Sensor**: 2:1 resistive divider conditioned output reading `1158.1 mV` on ESP32 GPIO34 (ADC1), matching physical DMM 1.17V benchmark. Reconstructs 2.316V sensor offset; flagged `uncalibrated` / null current per honest provenance rules until load test.
- **A7670E Cat-1 LTE Modem**: UART2 communication active, AT detected, registered on network (`registered yes`), packet data attached (`attached yes`), RSSI signal quality 22.
- **PZEM-017 DC Energy Meter**: UART1 Modbus RTU interface commissioned; displays `fault / unavailable` until 12V measurement power and micro-USB logic supply are simultaneously connected.
- **HW-548 MOSFET / 12V Siren**: GPIO27 active-HIGH output with hardware 5-second automatic cutoff timer. Direct bench command `siren_test` / `SIREN` integrated for one-click verification.
- **GPS NEO-6M**: Hardware connected on GPIO32 RX. Reports `fault` indoors because 1.5 GHz satellite signals require line-of-sight and cannot penetrate indoor concrete ceilings.
- **WebSerial & Next.js Telemetry Contract v2**: Ingests v2 NDJSON telemetry with `load_mode: "bench_resistor"` and `meter_source: "bench_supply"`.
Verdict: **Test 11 Concurrent all-module firmware and hardware integration operational on breadboard; transitioning to Final Phase Acceptance**.

### 18 October, GPIO19 correction and premature alarm cutoff repair

The user corrected their earlier report: the LED/resistor input had been connected to GND rather than GPIO19. After moving it to GPIO19, they report a brief light (approximately one second), rather than the requested five-second observation. This establishes visible GPIO19-driven LED activity per the user, not five-second timing or siren acceptance. The preceding instruction kept the siren 12 V supply disconnected. Read-only records after23:30 through23:37 show no retained manual-test reason, so the specific button/command received during this latest observation is not independently established. Running firmware still reports2.0.1.

Found a real firmware backup-cutoff bug: loop-start `now` can precede the `alarmSince=millis()` captured by a later command/contact trigger. Unsigned subtraction then wraps, falsely satisfying the five-second timeout and switching the output off immediately. This is a confirmed source defect, not proof that it caused the user's approximate one-second observation. Prepared2.0.2 now samples the cutoff clock after command/contact processing under the security lock, and uses a shared elapsed-time policy that rejects older clock samples while preserving rollover behavior. The independent esp_timer cutoff, fixed GPIO/polarity and configured duration remain unchanged. LED-only test retains300ms and now replies `# LED TEST requested 300 ms`; siren-test acknowledgment prints the actual configured duration (`# SIREN TEST requested 5000 ms` for the final configuration), so the two tests can be distinguished. GPIO state/acknowledgment is not physical siren feedback.

### 19 October — user screenshot: serial buffer limit with no accepted USB frames

The user supplied a screenshot showing115200 baud, an open USB port receiving bytes, `Serial buffer exceeded limit`, no last valid USB frame, and retained server report105 from18 October23:56:38. This is evidence that the previous website-only verification did not establish recovery of the user's actual stream. The selected all-axis option is a user selection; the new default remains one X-axis line on a fresh Lab session.

Compared current transport parsing with remote/main base7db97a3: both imposed a64KiB cap before consuming complete lines and only split on LF. Four new regression cases fail against that parser: a valid batch over64KiB loses all reports; an overlong line discards later valid reports in the same batch; an overlong line split across reads can wrongly accept its suffix as a new report; CR-only reports are not separated. Corrected to enforce the same64KiB bound per unfinished line, process complete CR/LF/CRLF-delimited reports, discard oversized lines through the next real boundary and resume subsequent valid frames. Strict JSON/schema/provenance validation is retained. These reproduced software defects do **not** establish which bytes or condition produced the user's actual screenshot.

Web Serial now opens explicitly as8N1/no flow control with a64KiB receive buffer. Added a bounded local receive diagnostic when the port is not streaming: total byte count, CR/LF counts, non-ASCII/control byte count, escaped final160 decoded characters and final24 bytes in hexadecimal. It is updated through a ref to avoid rendering per byte chunk. This distinguishes a line-framing failure from unexpected/garbled input without inventing live sensor values or repeatedly enlarging the parser limit. Retained server history is explicitly identified while waiting. No raw diagnostic samples are automatically uploaded.
